					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Partially recognised state in the South Caucasus</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the partially recognised state in the South Caucasus. For the ethnolinguistic region, see <a href="/wiki/Ossetia" title="Ossetia">Ossetia</a>. For the Georgian entity, see <a href="/wiki/Provisional_Administration_of_South_Ossetia" title="Provisional Administration of South Ossetia">Provisional Administration of South Ossetia</a>.</div>

<p><b>South Ossetia</b><sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;n 1&#93;</a></sup> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/df/Ossetia_pronunciation.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator style="font-size:90%">SET</span>-ee-ə</i></a>, <span style="font-size:85%;">less common:</span> <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="/ʃ/: &#39;sh&#39; in &#39;shy&#39;">ʃ</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-2" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/d\/d7\/Ossetia_alternate_pronunciation.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Ossetia alternate pronunciation.mp3&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d7/Ossetia_alternate_pronunciation.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Ossetia_alternate_pronunciation.mp3" title="File:Ossetia alternate pronunciation.mp3">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">o-<span style="font-size:90%">SEE</span>-shə</i></a>),<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Republic of South Ossetia – the State of Alania</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Abkhazia_and_South_Ossetia" title="International recognition of Abkhazia and South Ossetia">partially recognised</a><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked state</a> in the <a href="/wiki/South_Caucasus" title="South Caucasus">South Caucasus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> It has an officially stated population of just over 56,500 people (2022), who live in an area of 3,900 square kilometres (1,500&#160;sq&#160;mi), with 33,000 living in the capital city, <a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a>.
</p><p>As of 2023, only <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nauru" title="Nauru">Nauru</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a> recognise South Ossetia as a sovereign state.<sup id="cite_ref-recognitions_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-recognitions-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Georgia_(country)" title="Government of Georgia (country)">Georgian government</a> and nearly all <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> member states consider South Ossetia a <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_territory" class="mw-redirect" title="Sovereign territory">sovereign territory</a> of Georgia <a href="/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_in_Georgia" title="Russian-occupied territories in Georgia">occupied by Russia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The political status of South Ossetia is a central issue of the <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">Georgian–Ossetian conflict</a> and <a href="/wiki/Georgia%E2%80%93Russia_relations" title="Georgia–Russia relations">Georgia–Russia relations</a>. The Georgian constitution designates the area as "the former autonomous district of South Ossetia", in reference to the <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_Autonomous_Oblast" title="South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast">South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast</a> disbanded in 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Georgia_(country)" title="Government of Georgia (country)">Georgian government</a> informally refers to the area as the <b>Tskhinvali region</b><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;n 2&#93;</a></sup> and considers it a part of Georgia's <a href="/wiki/Shida_Kartli" title="Shida Kartli">Shida Kartli</a> region. Lacking effective control over the territory, Georgia maintains an administrative body called <a href="/wiki/Provisional_Administration_of_South_Ossetia" title="Provisional Administration of South Ossetia">Provisional Administration of South Ossetia</a>.
</p><p>The South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast, established by Soviet authorities in 1922, <a href="/wiki/Declaration_of_independence" title="Declaration of independence">declared independence</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Georgian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic">Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic</a> in 1991. The Georgian government responded by abolishing South Ossetia's autonomy and trying to re-establish its control over the region by force.<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The escalating crisis led to the <a href="/wiki/1991%E2%80%931992_South_Ossetia_War" class="mw-redirect" title="1991–1992 South Ossetia War">1991–1992 South Ossetia War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Georgians have fought against South Ossetians on two other occasions: in <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict#The_2004_flare-up" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">2004</a> and in <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict#Events_in_2008" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">2008</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-king_fivedaywar_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-king_fivedaywar-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The latter conflict led to the <a href="/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War" title="Russo-Georgian War">Russo-Georgian War</a> of August 2008, during which Ossetian and Russian forces gained full <i>de facto</i> control of the territory of the former South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast. Since the 2008 war, Georgia and a significant part of the <a href="/wiki/International_community" title="International community">international community</a> have regarded South Ossetia as <a href="/wiki/Occupied_territories_of_Georgia" class="mw-redirect" title="Occupied territories of Georgia">occupied by the Russian military</a>.
</p><p>South Ossetia relies heavily on military, political, and financial aid from Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_Army_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy_Army-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Since 2008, the South Ossetian government has expressed <a href="/wiki/Proposed_Russian_annexation_of_South_Ossetia" title="Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia">their intention of joining the Russian Federation</a>; if successful, this would end its proclaimed independence. The prospect of a referendum on this matter has been raised multiple times in domestic politics, but none have taken place.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ossetia" class="mw-redirect" title="History of Ossetia">History of Ossetia</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Medieval_and_early_modern_period">Medieval and early modern period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Medieval and early modern period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Colton,_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_(BB).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg/220px-Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="177" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg/330px-Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg/440px-Colton%2C_G.W._Turkey_In_Asia_And_The_Caucasian_Provinces_Of_Russia._1856_%28BB%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1233" data-file-height="993" /></a><figcaption>Fragment of 1856 map by <a href="/wiki/J._H._Colton" title="J. H. Colton">J. H. Colton</a> depicting <a href="/wiki/Caucasus" title="Caucasus">Caucasus</a> region. Modern South Ossetia is located below the green "Ossia", which approximately corresponds to modern North Ossetia.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Ossetians" title="Ossetians">Ossetians</a> are believed to originate from the <a href="/wiki/Alans" title="Alans">Alans</a>, a nomadic <a href="/wiki/Iranian_peoples" title="Iranian peoples">Iranian</a> tribe.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> In the 8th century a consolidated Alan kingdom, referred to in sources of the period as <a href="/wiki/Alania" title="Alania">Alania</a>, emerged in the northern Caucasus Mountains. Around 1239–1277 Alania fell to the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol</a> and later to the <a href="/wiki/Timur" title="Timur">Timur</a>'s armies, who massacred much of the Alanian population. The survivors among the Alans retreated into the mountains of the central Caucasus and gradually started migration to the south, across the Caucasus Mountains into the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Georgia" title="Kingdom of Georgia">Kingdom of Georgia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-toal_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-toal-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_Caucasus_War_(1809-1817)_by_Anosov.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg/220px-Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg/330px-Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg/440px-Map_Caucasus_War_%281809-1817%29_by_Anosov.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3096" data-file-height="2230" /></a><figcaption>Historical Russian map of the Caucasus region at the beginning of the 19th century</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the 17th century, by pressure of <a href="/wiki/Kabardians" title="Kabardians">Kabardian</a> princes, Ossetians started a second wave of migration from the <a href="/wiki/North_Caucasus" title="North Caucasus">North Caucasus</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kartli" title="Kingdom of Kartli">Kingdom of Kartli</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Ossetian peasants, who were migrating to the mountainous areas of the <a href="/wiki/South_Caucasus" title="South Caucasus">South Caucasus</a>, often settled in the lands of Georgian feudal lords.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199438_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199438-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The Georgian King of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kartli" title="Kingdom of Kartli">Kingdom of Kartli</a> permitted Ossetians to immigrate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199439_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199439-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> According to Russian ambassador to Georgia <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mikhail_Tatishchev&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mikhail Tatishchev (page does not exist)">Mikhail Tatishchev</a>, at the beginning of the 17th century there was already a small group of Ossetians living near the headwaters of the <a href="/wiki/Greater_Liakhvi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Greater Liakhvi River">Greater Liakhvi River</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199439_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199439-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In the 1770s there were more Ossetians living in <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kartli_(1484%E2%80%931762)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Kartli (1484–1762)">Kartli</a> than ever before.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ossetian_expansion.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Ossetian_expansion.svg/220px-Ossetian_expansion.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="207" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Ossetian_expansion.svg/330px-Ossetian_expansion.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Ossetian_expansion.svg/440px-Ossetian_expansion.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="375" data-file-height="352" /></a><figcaption>Ossetian migration over time</figcaption></figure>
<p>This period has been documented in the travel diaries of <a href="/wiki/Johann_Anton_G%C3%BCldenst%C3%A4dt" title="Johann Anton Güldenstädt">Johann Anton Güldenstädt</a> who visited Georgia in 1772. The Baltic German explorer called modern <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetia" class="mw-redirect" title="North Ossetia">North Ossetia</a> simply Ossetia, while he wrote that Kartli (the areas of modern-day South Ossetia) was populated by Georgians and the mountainous areas were populated by both Georgians and Ossetians.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199440_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199440-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Güldenstädt also wrote that the northernmost border of Kartli is the <a href="/wiki/Greater_Caucasus" title="Greater Caucasus">Major Caucasus Ridge</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199437_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199437-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> By the end of 18th century, the ultimate sites of Ossetian settlement on the territory of modern South Ossetia were in Kudaro (<a href="/wiki/Jejora" title="Jejora">Jejora</a> river estuary), <a href="/wiki/Greater_Liakhvi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Greater Liakhvi River">Greater Liakhvi</a> gorge, the gorge of <a href="/wiki/Little_Liakhvi" title="Little Liakhvi">Little Liakhvi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ksani" title="Ksani">Ksani</a> River gorge, Guda (<a href="/wiki/Aragvi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Aragvi River">Tetri Aragvi</a> estuary) and Truso (<a href="/wiki/Terek_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Terek River">Terek</a> estuary).<sup id="cite_ref-Historical_review_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Historical_review-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Georgian <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kartli-Kakheti" title="Kingdom of Kartli-Kakheti">Kingdom of Kartli-Kakheti</a>, including the territory of modern South Ossetia, was annexed by the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a> in 1801. Ossetia, the area comprising modern <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetia%E2%80%93Alania" title="North Ossetia–Alania">North Ossetia</a>, was among the first areas of the <a href="/wiki/North_Caucasus" title="North Caucasus">North Caucasus</a> to come under Russian domination, starting in 1774.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The capital, <a href="/wiki/Vladikavkaz" title="Vladikavkaz">Vladikavkaz</a>, was the first Russian military outpost in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> By 1806, Ossetia was completely under Russian control. Ossetian migration to Georgian areas continued in the 19th and 20th centuries, when <a href="/wiki/Georgia_within_the_Russian_Empire" title="Georgia within the Russian Empire">Georgia was part of the Russian Empire</a> and Ossetian settlements emerged in <a href="/wiki/Trialeti" title="Trialeti">Trialeti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Borjomi_Municipality" title="Borjomi Municipality">Borjomi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bakuriani" title="Bakuriani">Bakuriani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kakheti" title="Kakheti">Kakheti</a> as well.<sup id="cite_ref-Historical_review_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Historical_review-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1850 when Georgia was fully under the control of Russian Empire, Georgian powerful family <a href="/wiki/Machabeli" title="Machabeli">Machabeli</a> complained to Russian authorities about not being able to seize control over mountain gorges where the Ossetian populations lived.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Disputed_statement" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:Disputed statement"><span title="This claim has reliable sources with contradicting facts (February 2022)">disputed</span></a>&#32;(for: Claim cannot be found in given reference)&#160;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:South_Ossetia" title="Talk:South Ossetia">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> Here is what Russian officials, collegiate assessors Yanovsky and Kozachkovsky, wrote in 1831 about the relationship between Georgian feudal lords and the Ossetian population of mountain gorges in their “Notes on Ossetian gorges appropriated by the princes Eristov-Ksani”: “...<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>in more distant gorges, such as: Magrandoletsky, Tliysky, Chipransky, Gvidisk, Knogsky and others, for which the <a href="/wiki/Eristavi" title="Eristavi">Eristavi</a>'s declare a claim, there are no traces of their control. Before being conquered by our troops, the Ossetians living in these gorges were a model of primitive peoples. In the villages and gorges there was absolutely no order and obedience. Everyone who was able to carry weapons considered himself completely independent. The example of the Eristavi's gave rise to the princes of Machabeli to appropriate the newly conquered Ossetians living along the Bolshaya Liakhva, in the gorges of Roksky, Jomaksky, Urschuarsky, who never obeyed and did not belong to them."<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="South_Ossetia_as_a_part_of_the_Soviet_Union">South Ossetia as a part of the Soviet Union</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: South Ossetia as a part of the Soviet Union">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg/220px-Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg/330px-Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg/440px-Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia_map.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1213" data-file-height="850" /></a><figcaption>Democratic Republic of Georgia (1918–1921) in 1921</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following the <a href="/wiki/October_Revolution" title="October Revolution">Russian revolution</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouleimanov201399_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouleimanov201399-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> the area of modern South Ossetia became part of the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_Georgia" title="Democratic Republic of Georgia">Democratic Republic of Georgia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In 1918, <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict_(1918%E2%80%9320)" class="mw-redirect" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict (1918–20)">conflict began</a> between the landless Ossetian peasants living in <a href="/wiki/Shida_Kartli" title="Shida Kartli">Shida Kartli</a> (Interior Georgia), who were influenced by <a href="/wiki/Bolshevism" title="Bolshevism">Bolshevism</a> and demanded ownership of the lands they worked, and the <a href="/wiki/Mensheviks" title="Mensheviks">Menshevik government</a> backed ethnic Georgian aristocrats, who were legal owners. Although the Ossetians were initially discontented with the economic policies of the central government, the tension soon transformed into ethnic conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> The first Ossetian rebellion began in February 1918, when three Georgian princes were killed and their land was seized by the Ossetians. The central government of <a href="/wiki/Tbilisi" title="Tbilisi">Tiflis</a> retaliated by sending the National Guard to the area. However, the Georgian unit retreated after they had engaged the Ossetians.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Ossetian rebels then proceeded to occupy the town of <a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a> and began attacking the ethnic Georgian civilian population. During uprisings in 1919 and 1920, the Ossetians were covertly supported by <a href="/wiki/Russian_Soviet_Federative_Socialist_Republic" title="Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic">Soviet Russia</a>, but even so, were defeated.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113_37-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013112–113-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> According to allegations made by Ossetian sources, the crushing of the 1920 uprising caused the death of 5,000 Ossetians, while ensuing hunger and epidemics were the causes of death of more than 13,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg/220px-Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="204" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg/330px-Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg/440px-Creation_of_South_Ossetia_in_1922.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="447" data-file-height="415" /></a><figcaption>Creation of South Ossetian AO on historical Georgian regions in 1922</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Soviet-caucasus1922.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Soviet-caucasus1922.png/220px-Soviet-caucasus1922.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Soviet-caucasus1922.png/330px-Soviet-caucasus1922.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Soviet-caucasus1922.png/440px-Soviet-caucasus1922.png 2x" data-file-width="1613" data-file-height="1090" /></a><figcaption>Map of the <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_Autonomous_Oblast" title="South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast">South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast</a> in 1922</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png/220px-Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="116" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png/330px-Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png/440px-Georgian_soviet_republic1957_1991.png 2x" data-file-width="1461" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>Map of the <a href="/wiki/Georgian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic">Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic</a> in 1957–1991</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Soviet Georgian government, established after the <a href="/wiki/Red_Army_invasion_of_Georgia" title="Red Army invasion of Georgia">Red Army invasion of Georgia</a> in 1921, created an autonomous administrative unit for <a href="/wiki/Transcaucasia" class="mw-redirect" title="Transcaucasia">Transcaucasian</a> Ossetians in April 1922 under pressure from <a href="/wiki/Kavbiuro" title="Kavbiuro">Kavbiuro</a> (the Caucasian Bureau of the Central Committee of the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Communist Party of the Soviet Union">Communist Party of the Soviet Union</a>), called the <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_Autonomous_Oblast" title="South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast">South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast (AO)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question1994153–161_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question1994153–161-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Some believe that the <a href="/wiki/Bolsheviks" title="Bolsheviks">Bolsheviks</a> granted this autonomy to the Ossetians in exchange for their (<a href="/wiki/Bolsheviks" title="Bolsheviks">Bolshevik</a>) loyalty in fighting the Democratic Republic of Georgia and favouring local separatists, since this area had never been a separate entity prior to the Russian invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-roudik_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roudik-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The drawing of administrative boundaries of the South Ossetian AO was quite a complicated process. Many Georgian villages were included within the South Ossetian AO despite numerous protests by the Georgian population. While the city of <a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a> did not have a majority Ossetian population, it was made the capital of the South Ossetian AO.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question1994153–161_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question1994153–161-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> In addition to parts of <a href="/wiki/Gori_Uyezd" class="mw-redirect" title="Gori Uyezd">Gori Uyezd</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dusheti_Uyezd" class="mw-redirect" title="Dusheti Uyezd">Dusheti Uyezd</a> of <a href="/wiki/Tiflis_Governorate" title="Tiflis Governorate">Tiflis Governorate</a>, parts of <a href="/wiki/Racha_Uyezd" class="mw-redirect" title="Racha Uyezd">Racha Uyezd</a> of <a href="/wiki/Kutaisi_Governorate" title="Kutaisi Governorate">Kutaisi Governorate</a> (western Georgia) were also included within the South Ossetian AO. All these territories historically had been indigenous Georgian lands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199472–73_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199472–73-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Historical Ossetia in the North Caucasus did not have its own political entity before 1924, when the <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetian_Autonomous_Oblast" title="North Ossetian Autonomous Oblast">North Ossetian Autonomous Oblast</a> was created.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199472–73_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199472–73-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although the Ossetians had their own language (<a href="/wiki/Ossetic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Ossetic language">Ossetian</a>), Russian and <a href="/wiki/Georgian_language" title="Georgian language">Georgian</a> were administrative/state languages.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated2_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Under the rule of Georgia's government during Soviet times, Ossetians enjoyed minority cultural autonomy, including speaking the Ossetian language and teaching it in schools.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated2_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> In 1989, two-thirds of Ossetians in the <a href="/wiki/Georgian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic">Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic</a> lived outside the South Ossetian AO.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199442_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOssetian_Question199442-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Georgian-Ossetian_conflict">Georgian-Ossetian conflict</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Georgian-Ossetian conflict">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">Georgian–Ossetian conflict</a></div>
<h4><span id="1989.E2.80.932008"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="1989–2008">1989–2008</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 1989–2008">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Tensions in the region began to rise amid rising nationalism among both Georgians and Ossetians in 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Before this, the two communities of the <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_Autonomous_Oblast" title="South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast">South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast</a> of the Georgian SSR had been living in peace with each other except for the <a href="/wiki/Georgian-Ossetian_conflict_(1918-1920)" class="mw-redirect" title="Georgian-Ossetian conflict (1918-1920)">1918–1920 events</a>. Both ethnicities had a normal degree of interaction and there were many Georgian-Ossetian intermarriages.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The dispute surrounding the presence of the Ossetian people in the <a href="/wiki/South_Caucasus" title="South Caucasus">South Caucasus</a> has been one of the causes of conflict. Although Georgian historiography believes that Ossetian mass migration to the South Caucasus (Georgia) began in the 17th century, Ossetians claim to have been residing in the area since the ancient times.,<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> which is not supported by available sources.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013119_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouleimanov2013119-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Some Ossetian historians accept that the migration of Ossetian ancestors to modern South Ossetia began after the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_invasions_of_Georgia_and_Armenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongol invasions of Georgia and Armenia">Mongol invasions</a> of the 13th century, while one South Ossetian <i>de facto</i> foreign minister in the 1990s said that the Ossetians first appeared in the area only in the early 17th century.<sup id="cite_ref-books.google.com_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-books.google.com-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Since it was created after the Russian invasion of 1921, South Ossetia was regarded as artificial creation by Georgians during the Soviet era.<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The South Ossetian Popular Front (<i>Ademon Nykhas</i>) was created in 1988. On 10 November 1989, the South Ossetian regional council asked the Georgian Supreme Council to upgrade the region to the status of an "<a href="/wiki/Autonomous_republics_of_the_Soviet_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Autonomous republics of the Soviet Union">autonomous republic</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The decision to transform the South Ossetian AO into the South Ossetian <a href="/wiki/Autonomous_Soviet_Socialist_Republics_of_the_Soviet_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Autonomous Soviet Socialist Republics of the Soviet Union">ASSR</a> by the South Ossetian authorities escalated the conflict. On 11 November, this decision was revoked by the Georgian parliament, the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Soviet_of_the_Georgian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Supreme Soviet of the Georgian Soviet Socialist Republic">Supreme Soviet</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The Georgian authorities removed the First Party Secretary of the oblast from his position.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Georgian Supreme Council adopted a law barring regional parties in summer 1990. The South Ossetian regional council interpreted this as a move against Ademon Nykhas and subsequently passed a "declaration of national sovereignty", proclaiming the South Ossetian Soviet Democratic Republic within the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> on 20 September 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-SO_timeline_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SO_timeline-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Ossetians boycotted subsequent Georgian parliamentary elections and held their own contest in December.<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 1990, the parliamentary election in Georgia was won by <a href="/wiki/Zviad_Gamsakhurdia" title="Zviad Gamsakhurdia">Zviad Gamsakhurdia</a>'s "Round Table" block.<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> On 11 December 1990, Zviad Gamsakhurdia's government declared the Ossetian election illegitimate and abolished South Ossetia's autonomous status altogether.<sup id="cite_ref-icg-avoidwar_15-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icg-avoidwar-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Gamsakhurdia rationalised the abolition of Ossetian autonomy by saying, "They [Ossetians] have no right to a state here in Georgia. They are a national minority. Their homeland is North Ossetia<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>.... Here they are newcomers."<sup id="cite_ref-books.google.com_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-books.google.com-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When the Georgian parliament declared a state of emergency in the territory of South Ossetian AO on 12 December 1990, troops from both Georgian and Soviet interior ministries were sent to the region. After the Georgian National Guard was formed in early 1991, Georgian troops entered Tskhinvali on 5 January 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-dk_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dk-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/1991%E2%80%931992_South_Ossetia_War" class="mw-redirect" title="1991–1992 South Ossetia War">1991–1992 South Ossetia War</a> was characterised by general disregard for international humanitarian law by uncontrollable militias, with both sides reporting atrocities.<sup id="cite_ref-dk_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dk-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The Soviet military facilitated a ceasefire as ordered by <a href="/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev" title="Mikhail Gorbachev">Mikhail Gorbachev</a> in January 1991. In March and April 1991, Soviet interior troops were reported actively disarming militias on both sides, and deterring the inter-ethnic violence. Zviad Gamsakhurdia asserted that the Soviet leadership was encouraging South Ossetian separatism in order to force Georgia not to leave the Soviet Union. Georgia declared its independence in April 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-princeton_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-princeton-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-books.google.com_51-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-books.google.com-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a result of the war, about 100,000 ethnic Ossetians fled the territory and Georgia proper, most across the border into North Ossetia. A further 23,000 ethnic Georgians fled South Ossetia to other parts of Georgia.<sup id="cite_ref-hrw1996-ingush_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hrw1996-ingush-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Many refugees went to the North Ossetian <a href="/wiki/Prigorodny_District,_North_Ossetia%E2%80%93Alania" title="Prigorodny District, North Ossetia–Alania">Prigorodnyi District</a>. In 1944, many South Ossetians were resettled in areas of North Ossetia from which the <a href="/wiki/Ingush_people" title="Ingush people">Ingush</a> had been expelled by <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Stalin" title="Joseph Stalin">Stalin</a> in 1944. In the 1990s the new wave of South Ossetians migrating to the former Ingush territory fuelled conflict between Ossetians and Ingush.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 29 April 1991, the western part of South Ossetia was affected by an <a href="/wiki/1991_Racha_earthquake" title="1991 Racha earthquake">earthquake</a>, which killed more than 200 and left tens of thousands homeless.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In late 1991, <a href="/wiki/Georgian_Civil_War#Civil_unrest" title="Georgian Civil War">dissent</a> was mounting against Gamsakhurdia in Georgia due to his intolerance of critics and attempts to concentrate political power.<sup id="cite_ref-princeton_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-princeton-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> On 22 December 1991, after a <a href="/wiki/1991%E2%80%9392_Georgian_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" class="mw-redirect" title="1991–92 Georgian coup d&#39;état">coup d'état</a>, Gamsakhurdia and his supporters were besieged by the opposition, which was backed by the national guard, in several government buildings in <a href="/wiki/Tbilisi" title="Tbilisi">Tbilisi</a>. The ensuing heavy fighting resulted in over 200 casualties and left the center of the Georgian capital in ruins. On 6 January, Gamsakhurdia and several of his supporters fled the city for exile. Afterwards, the <a href="/wiki/Military_Council_(Georgia)" title="Military Council (Georgia)">Georgian military council</a>, an interim government, was formed by a triumvirate of <a href="/wiki/Jaba_Ioseliani" title="Jaba Ioseliani">Jaba Ioseliani</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tengiz_Kitovani" title="Tengiz Kitovani">Tengiz Kitovani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tengiz_Sigua" title="Tengiz Sigua">Tengiz Sigua</a>, and, in March 1992, they invited <a href="/wiki/Eduard_Shevardnadze" title="Eduard Shevardnadze">Eduard Shevardnadze</a>, a former Soviet minister, to come to Georgia to assume control of the Georgian State Council.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-engels_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-engels-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An <a href="/wiki/Independence_referendum" title="Independence referendum">independence referendum</a> was held in South Ossetia on 19 January 1992,<sup id="cite_ref-DD_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DD-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> with voters being asked two questions: "Do you agree that South Ossetia should be an independent country?" and "Do you agree with the South Ossetian parliament's solution of 1 September 1991 on reunion with Russia?"<sup id="cite_ref-DD_67-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DD-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Both proposals were approved,<sup id="cite_ref-DD_67-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DD-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> but the results were not recognised internationally.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, the South Ossetian regional council subsequently passed an "act of state independence" and declared the independence of the Republic of South Ossetia on 29 May 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-SO_timeline_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SO_timeline-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:1993_Georgia_war2.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/1993_Georgia_war2.svg/220px-1993_Georgia_war2.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/1993_Georgia_war2.svg/330px-1993_Georgia_war2.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/1993_Georgia_war2.svg/440px-1993_Georgia_war2.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1940" data-file-height="1370" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Georgian_Civil_War" title="Georgian Civil War">Georgian Civil War</a> from October to December 1993</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 24 June 1992, Shevardnadze and the South Ossetian government signed the <a href="/wiki/Sochi_agreement" title="Sochi agreement">Sochi ceasefire agreement</a>, brokered by Russia. The agreement included obligations to avoid the use of force, and Georgia pledged not to impose sanctions against South Ossetia. The Georgian government retained control over substantial portions of South Ossetia,<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> including the town of <a href="/wiki/Akhalgori" title="Akhalgori">Akhalgori</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Joint_Control_Commission_for_Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_Conflict_Resolution" title="Joint Control Commission for Georgian–Ossetian Conflict Resolution">Joined Peacekeeping force</a> of Ossetians, Russians and Georgians was established. On 6 November 1992, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe">Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe</a> (OSCE) set up a mission in Georgia to monitor the peacekeeping operation. From then until mid-2004, South Ossetia was generally peaceful.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the 2003 <a href="/wiki/Rose_Revolution" title="Rose Revolution">Rose Revolution</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mikheil_Saakashvili" title="Mikheil Saakashvili">Mikheil Saakashvili</a> became the President of Georgia in 2004. Ahead of the 2004 parliamentary and presidential elections, he promised to restore the territorial integrity of Georgia.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> During one of his early speeches, Saakashvili addressed the separatist regions, saying, "[N]either Georgia nor its president will put up with disintegration of Georgia. Therefore, we offer immediate negotiations to our Abkhazian and Ossetian friends. We are ready to discuss every model of statehood by taking into consideration their interests for the promotion of their future development."<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 2004, tensions began to rise as the Georgian authorities strengthened their efforts to bring the region back under their rule, after it succeeded in <a href="/wiki/2004_Adjara_crisis" title="2004 Adjara crisis">Adjara</a>. Georgia sent police to close down the Ergneti black market, which was one of the region's chief sources of revenue, selling foodstuffs and fuel smuggled from Russia. The Georgian authorities claimed the massive smuggling of goods for the Ergneti market through the <a href="/wiki/Roki_Tunnel" title="Roki Tunnel">Roki Tunnel</a>, which was not under Georgian control, cost the country significant amounts of custom revenues.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Georgia proposed to bring the Roki tunnel under joint control and monitoring, which was refused by the South Ossetian side.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The antismuggling operation against the market resulted in a breakdown of South Ossetian trust in Georgia's intentions.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> A wave of violence erupted between Georgian peacekeepers and South Ossetian militiamen and freelance fighters from Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2004timeline_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2004timeline-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> This included hostage taking of dozens of Georgian peacekeepers,<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> shootouts and shelling of Georgian controlled villages, which left dozens dead and wounded. A ceasefire deal was reached on 13 August though it was repeatedly violated.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2004timeline_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2004timeline-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Georgian government protested against the allegedly increasing Russian economic and political presence in the region and against the uncontrolled military of the South Ossetian side.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Georgian government officials have stated South Ossetian key security positions are occupied by (former) Russian security officials,<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> while some political researchers speak of institutions being outsourced to the Russian Federation.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It also considered the <a href="/wiki/Joint_Control_Commission_for_Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_Conflict_Resolution" title="Joint Control Commission for Georgian–Ossetian Conflict Resolution">peacekeeping force</a> (consisting in equal parts of South Ossetians, North Ossetians, Russians and Georgians) to be non-neutral and demanded its replacement. Various proposals were launched by the Georgian side to internationalise the peacekeeping in South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-osce2015_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-osce2015-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> According to U.S. senator <a href="/wiki/Richard_Lugar" title="Richard Lugar">Richard Lugar</a>, the United States supported Georgia's call in 2006 for the withdrawal of Russian peacekeepers from the conflict zones.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Later, EU South Caucasus envoy <a href="/wiki/Peter_Semneby" title="Peter Semneby">Peter Semneby</a> said that "Russia's actions in the <a href="/wiki/2006_Georgian-Russian_espionage_controversy" class="mw-redirect" title="2006 Georgian-Russian espionage controversy">Georgia spy row</a> have damaged its credibility as a neutral peacekeeper in the EU's Black Sea neighbourhood."<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Biden" class="mw-redirect" title="Joseph Biden">Joseph Biden</a> (Chairman, U.S. Senate Foreign Relations Committee), <a href="/wiki/Richard_Lugar" title="Richard Lugar">Richard Lugar</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mel_Martinez" class="mw-redirect" title="Mel Martinez">Mel Martinez</a> sponsored a resolution in June 2008 accusing Russia of attempting to undermine Georgia's territorial integrity and called for replacing the Russian-manned peacekeeping force operating under CIS mandate.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="2008_war">2008 war</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 2008 war">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War" title="Russo-Georgian War">Russo-Georgian War</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:SO1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SO1.jpg/220px-SO1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SO1.jpg/330px-SO1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SO1.jpg/440px-SO1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1085" data-file-height="804" /></a><figcaption>South Ossetia before the war</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/2008_Russo-Georgian_diplomatic_crisis" title="2008 Russo-Georgian diplomatic crisis">Tensions between Georgia and Russia began escalating</a> in April 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-shelling_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shelling-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tall_order_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tall_order-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> A bomb explosion on 1 August 2008 targeted a car transporting Georgian peacekeepers. South Ossetians were responsible for instigating this incident, which marked the opening of hostilities and injured five Georgian servicemen. In response,<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> several South Ossetian militiamen were hit.<sup id="cite_ref-feud_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-feud-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> South Ossetian separatists began shelling Georgian villages on 1 August. These artillery attacks caused Georgian servicemen to return fire periodically from 1 August.<sup id="cite_ref-shelling_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shelling-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-feud_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-feud-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Allison_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allison-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At around 19:00 on 7 August 2008, Georgian president <a href="/wiki/Mikheil_Saakashvili" title="Mikheil Saakashvili">Mikheil Saakashvili</a> announced a unilateral <a href="/wiki/Ceasefire" title="Ceasefire">ceasefire</a> and called for peace talks.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> However, escalating assaults against Georgian villages (located in the South Ossetian conflict zone) were soon matched with gunfire from Georgian troops,<sup id="cite_ref-Jamestown_Goals_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jamestown_Goals-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> who then proceeded to move in the direction of the capital of the <a href="/wiki/Self-proclaimed" title="Self-proclaimed">self-proclaimed</a> Republic of South Ossetia (<a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a>) on the night of 8 August, reaching its center in the morning of 8 August.<sup id="cite_ref-washingtonpost.com_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-washingtonpost.com-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> One Georgian diplomat told Russian newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/Kommersant" title="Kommersant">Kommersant</a></i> on 8 August that by taking control of Tskhinvali, <a href="/wiki/Tbilisi" title="Tbilisi">Tbilisi</a> wanted to demonstrate that Georgia would not tolerate the killing of Georgian citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-rmd_source_1_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rmd_source_1-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> According to Russian military expert <a href="/wiki/Pavel_Felgenhauer" title="Pavel Felgenhauer">Pavel Felgenhauer</a>, the Ossetian provocation was aimed at triggering the Georgian response, which was needed as a <a href="/wiki/Pretext" title="Pretext">pretext</a> for premeditated Russian military invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> According to Georgian intelligence,<sup id="cite_ref-NYTFreshev_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYTFreshev-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> and several Russian media reports, parts of the regular (non-peacekeeping) Russian Army had already moved to South Ossetian territory through the <a href="/wiki/Roki_Tunnel" title="Roki Tunnel">Roki Tunnel</a> before the Georgian military action.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Russia accused Georgia of "aggression against South Ossetia",<sup id="cite_ref-roudik_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-roudik-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> and launched a large-scale land, air, and sea invasion of Georgia with the pretext of a "<a href="/wiki/Peace_enforcement" title="Peace enforcement">peace enforcement</a> operation" on 8 August 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-Allison_103-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allison-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Russian <a href="/wiki/Airstrike" title="Airstrike">airstrikes</a> against targets within Georgia were also launched.<sup id="cite_ref-radar_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-radar-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Abkhazia" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Abkhazia">Abkhaz forces</a> opened a second front on 9 August by <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Kodori_Valley" title="Battle of the Kodori Valley">attacking the Kodori Gorge</a>, held by Georgia.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Tskhinvali was seized by the Russian military by 10 August.<sup id="cite_ref-radar_113-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-radar-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Russian forces occupied the Georgian cities of <a href="/wiki/Zugdidi" title="Zugdidi">Zugdidi</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-new_front_deeper_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-new_front_deeper-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Senaki" title="Senaki">Senaki</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-gori_central_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gori_central-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Poti" title="Poti">Poti</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Russia_NYTimes_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Russia_NYTimes-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Gori,_Georgia" title="Gori, Georgia">Gori</a> (the last one after the ceasefire agreement was negotiated).<sup id="cite_ref-gori_major_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gori_major-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The Russian <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea_Fleet" title="Black Sea Fleet">Black Sea Fleet</a> blockaded the Georgian coast.<sup id="cite_ref-Allison_103-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allison-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg/220px-Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg/330px-Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg/440px-Dom_v_Tshinvali_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Tskhinvali in August 2008</figcaption></figure>
<p>A campaign of <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing_of_Georgians_in_South_Ossetia" title="Ethnic cleansing of Georgians in South Ossetia">ethnic cleansing against Georgians in South Ossetia</a> was conducted by South Ossetians,<sup id="cite_ref-Fact-finding_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fact-finding-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> with Georgian villages around Tskhinvali being destroyed after the war had ended.<sup id="cite_ref-Damage_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Damage-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The war displaced 192,000 people,<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> and while many were able to return to their homes after the war, a year later around 30,000 ethnic Georgians remained displaced.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> In an interview published in <i>Kommersant</i>, South Ossetian leader <a href="/wiki/Eduard_Kokoity" title="Eduard Kokoity">Eduard Kokoity</a> said he would not allow Georgians to return.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/President_of_France" title="President of France">President of France</a> <a href="/wiki/Nicolas_Sarkozy" title="Nicolas Sarkozy">Nicolas Sarkozy</a> negotiated a ceasefire agreement on 12 August 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> On 17 August, Russian president <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev" title="Dmitry Medvedev">Dmitry Medvedev</a> announced that Russian forces would begin to pull out of Georgia the following day.<sup id="cite_ref-http_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-http-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Russia recognised Abkhazia and South Ossetia as separate republics on 26 August.<sup id="cite_ref-kremlin_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kremlin-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In response to Russia's recognition, the Georgian government severed diplomatic relations with Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-break_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-break-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Russian forces left the buffer areas bordering <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a> and South Ossetia on 8 October and the <a href="/wiki/European_Union_Monitoring_Mission" class="mw-redirect" title="European Union Monitoring Mission">European Union Monitoring Mission</a> in Georgia assumed authority over the buffer areas.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Since the war, Georgia has maintained that Abkhazia and South Ossetia are <a href="/wiki/Occupied_territories_of_Georgia" class="mw-redirect" title="Occupied territories of Georgia">Russian-occupied Georgian territories</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-parliament.ge_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parliament.ge-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 30 September 2009, the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>–sponsored <i>Independent International Fact-Finding Mission on the Conflict in Georgia</i> stated that, while preceded by months of mutual provocations, "open hostilities began with a large-scale Georgian military operation against the town of Tskhinvali and the surrounding areas, launched in the night of 7 to 8 August 2008."<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="After_the_2008_war">After the 2008 war</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: After the 2008 war">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2016, a referendum on integration with Russia was proposed during an election campaign, but was put on hold indefinitely.<sup id="cite_ref-referendum16_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-referendum16-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/2017_South_Ossetian_name_change_referendum" title="2017 South Ossetian name change referendum">referendum on South Ossetia's official name</a> was held on 9 April 2017; over three-quarters of those who voted supported amendments to the South Ossetian constitution which gave the names "Republic of South Ossetia" and "State of Alania" equal status under the law.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>President <a href="/wiki/Anatoly_Bibilov" title="Anatoly Bibilov">Anatoly Bibilov</a> announced on 26 March 2022 that <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_South_Ossetia" title="Armed Forces of South Ossetia">South Ossetian troops</a> had been sent to assist Russia in <a href="/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine">its invasion of Ukraine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-HelpProtectRussia_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HelpProtectRussia-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BreakawaySouthOssetia_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BreakawaySouthOssetia-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Bibilov announced on 30 March 2022 that South Ossetia would initiate the legal process to <a href="/wiki/Proposed_Russian_annexation_of_South_Ossetia" title="Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia">become part of Russia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-PretensesDropped_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PretensesDropped-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> Russian politicians reacted positively and said Russian law would permit (parts of) foreign nations to join the federation. They highlighted the necessity to "express the will of the Ossetian people" through a referendum.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Ossetian leader Bibilov said in a lengthy interview that he planned to hold two referendums, one on annexation by Russia, and the second vote on joining North Ossetia,<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> for which he set the election proceedings in motion on April 7, 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> On May 13, the annexation referendum was scheduled to take place on July 17.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Following Biblov's defeat in <a href="/wiki/2022_South_Ossetian_presidential_election" title="2022 South Ossetian presidential election">2022 election</a>, the new president, <a href="/wiki/Alan_Gagloyev" class="mw-redirect" title="Alan Gagloyev">Alan Gagloyev</a>, suspended the referendum on May 30.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Gagloyev announced in August 2022 that border crossings with Georgia would be open ten days a month.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography<span class="anchor" id="Geography"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Georgia_(country)" title="Geography of Georgia (country)">Geography of Georgia (country)</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Georgia_high_detail_map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Georgia_high_detail_map.png/220px-Georgia_high_detail_map.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Georgia_high_detail_map.png/330px-Georgia_high_detail_map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Georgia_high_detail_map.png/440px-Georgia_high_detail_map.png 2x" data-file-width="2529" data-file-height="1872" /></a><figcaption>Map of Georgia highlighting South Ossetia (purple) and <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a> (green)</figcaption></figure>
<p>South Ossetia is a very mountainous region located in the <a href="/wiki/Caucasus" title="Caucasus">Caucasus</a> at the juncture of Asia and Europe. It occupies the southern slopes of the <a href="/wiki/Greater_Caucasus" title="Greater Caucasus">Greater Caucasus</a> mountain range and its foothills which are part of the <a href="/wiki/Caucasian_Iberia" class="mw-redirect" title="Caucasian Iberia">Iberia Plain</a>, a geographic plateau that is roughly in the center of South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Likhi_Range" title="Likhi Range">Likhi Range</a> shapes the western geographic boundary of South Ossetia, although the northwestern corner of South Ossetia is located west of the range.
</p><p>The Greater Caucasus Mountain Range forms the northern border of South Ossetia with Russia. There is only one main road through the mountain range from South Ossetia to Russia, the <a href="/wiki/Transcaucasian_Highway" title="Transcaucasian Highway">TransKAM highway</a> through the <a href="/wiki/Roki_tunnel" class="mw-redirect" title="Roki tunnel">Roki tunnel</a> into <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetia%E2%80%93Alania" title="North Ossetia–Alania">North Ossetia</a>, which was completed in 1986. The Transkam section located in South Ossetia is nominally part of the Georgian <a href="/wiki/S10_highway_(Georgia)" title="S10 highway (Georgia)">S10 highway</a> even though Tbilisi effectively does not control that part. The Roki Tunnel was vital for the <a href="/wiki/Russian_military" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian military">Russian military</a> in the <a href="/wiki/2008_South_Ossetia_war" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 South Ossetia war">2008 South Ossetia war</a> because it is the only direct route through the Caucasus Mountains between Russia and South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>South Ossetia covers an area of about 3,900&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1,506&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> separated by the mountains from the more populous North Ossetia (which is a republic within Russia) and extending southwards almost to the <a href="/wiki/Mtkvari" class="mw-redirect" title="Mtkvari">Mtkvari</a> river in Georgia. More than 89% of South Ossetia lies over 1,000&#160;m (3,281&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above sea level</a>, and its highest point is <a href="/wiki/Mount_Khalatsa" title="Mount Khalatsa">Mount Khalatsa</a> at 3,938&#160;m (12,920&#160;ft) above sea level.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Out of the roughly 2,000 glaciers that exist in the Greater Caucasus, approximately <a href="/wiki/Glaciers_of_Georgia" title="Glaciers of Georgia">30% are located within Georgia</a>. The 10 glaciers of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Liakhvi" title="Great Liakhvi">Liakhvi River</a> basin and a handful of the <a href="/wiki/Rioni" title="Rioni">Rioni</a> River basin are located in South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most of South Ossetia is in the <a href="/wiki/Kura_(river)" title="Kura (river)">Kura Basin</a> with the northwestern part it in the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> basin. The <a href="/wiki/Likhi_Range" title="Likhi Range">Likhi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Racha" title="Racha">Racha</a> ridges act as divide separating these two <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">basins</a>. Major rivers in South Ossetia include the <a href="/wiki/Greater_Liakhvi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Greater Liakhvi River">Greater</a> and Little Liakhvi, <a href="/wiki/Ksani" title="Ksani">Ksani</a>, Medzhuda, Tlidon, Canal Saltanis, Ptsa River and host of other tributaries.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png/220px-Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="202" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png/330px-Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png/440px-Osetia_Po%C5%82udniowa.png 2x" data-file-width="2681" data-file-height="2466" /></a><figcaption>Topographic map of South Ossetia (<a href="/wiki/Polish_orthography" title="Polish orthography">Polish</a> transcription)</figcaption></figure>
<p>South Ossetia's climate is affected by <a href="/wiki/Subtropical_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Subtropical climate">subtropical</a> influences from the East and <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_climate" title="Mediterranean climate">Mediterranean</a> influences from the West. The Greater Caucasus range moderates the local climate by serving as a barrier against cold air from the north, which results in the fact that, even at great heights, it is warmer there than in the Northern Caucasus.<sup id="cite_ref-About_the_Republic_of_South_Ossetia_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-About_the_Republic_of_South_Ossetia-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-South_Ossetia_Maps_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-South_Ossetia_Maps-159">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Climatic zones in South Ossetia are determined by distance from the Black Sea and by altitude. The plains of eastern Georgia are shielded from the influence of the Black Sea by mountains that provide a more <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">continental climate</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The foothills and mountainous areas (including the Greater Caucasus Mountains) experience cool, wet summers and snowy winters, with snow cover often exceeding two meters in many regions. The penetration of humid air masses from the Black Sea to the west of South Ossetia is often blocked by the <a href="/wiki/Likhi_Range" title="Likhi Range">Likhi</a> mountain range. The wettest periods of the year in South Ossetia generally occur during spring and autumn while the winter and summer months tend to be the driest. Elevation plays an important role in South Ossetia where climatic conditions above 1,500&#160;m (4,921&#160;ft) are considerably colder than in any lower-lying areas. The regions that lie above 2,000&#160;m (6,562&#160;ft) frequently experience frost even during the summer months.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The average temperature in South Ossetia in January is around +4 degrees Celsius, and the average temperature in July is around +20.3 degrees Celsius. The average yearly liquid precipitation in South Ossetia is around 598 millimetres.<sup id="cite_ref-About_the_Republic_of_South_Ossetia_158-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-About_the_Republic_of_South_Ossetia-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> In general, summer temperatures average 20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F) to 24&#160;°C (75.2&#160;°F) across much of South Ossetia, and winter temperatures average 2&#160;°C (35.6&#160;°F) to 4&#160;°C (39.2&#160;°F). Humidity is relatively low and rainfall across South Ossetia averages 500 to 800&#160;mm (19.7 to 31.5&#160;in) per year, but <a href="/wiki/Alpine_climate" title="Alpine climate">Alpine</a> and highland regions have distinct <a href="/wiki/Microclimates" class="mw-redirect" title="Microclimates">microclimates</a>. At higher elevations, precipitation is sometimes twice as heavy as in the eastern plains of Georgia. Alpine conditions begin at about 2,100&#160;m (6,890&#160;ft), and above 3,600&#160;m (11,811&#160;ft) snow and ice are present year-round.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Encroachment">Encroachment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Encroachment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
