<p>The Russian troops who patrol the borders of South Ossetia have reportedly been expanding the boundaries of the region through “encroaching occupation”, meaning they surreptitiously advance several feet at a time into Georgian-held territory.<sup id="cite_ref-encroach_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-encroach-160">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_status">Political status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Political status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Abkhazia_and_South_Ossetia" title="International recognition of Abkhazia and South Ossetia">International recognition of Abkhazia and South Ossetia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_South_Ossetia" title="Foreign relations of South Ossetia">Foreign relations of South Ossetia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png/170px-Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="240" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png/255px-Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png/340px-Decree_recognising_South_Ossetia_independence.png 2x" data-file-width="2481" data-file-height="3508" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Ukaz" class="mw-redirect" title="Ukaz">Russian Presidential Decree</a> No. 1261 recognising South Ossetian independence</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following the <a href="/wiki/2008_South_Ossetia_war" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 South Ossetia war">2008 South Ossetia war</a>, Russia recognised South Ossetia as independent.<sup id="cite_ref-join_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-join-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> This unilateral recognition by Russia was met by condemnation from <a href="/wiki/Western_world" title="Western world">Western</a> Blocs, such as <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> (OSCE) and the <a href="/wiki/European_Council" title="European Council">European Council</a> due to the violation of Georgia's territorial integrity.<sup id="cite_ref-news.bbc.co.uk_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-news.bbc.co.uk-162">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-civil.ge_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-civil.ge-163">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceB_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceB-165">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> The EU's diplomatic response to the news was delayed by disagreements between eastern European states, the UK wanting a harsher response and Germany, France and other states' desire not to isolate Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Former US envoy <a href="/wiki/Richard_Holbrooke" title="Richard Holbrooke">Richard Holbrooke</a> said the conflict could encourage separatist movements in other former Soviet states along Russia's western border.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> Several days later, Nicaragua became the second country to recognise South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-join_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-join-161">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a> recognised South Ossetia on 10 September 2009, becoming the third UN member state to do so.<sup id="cite_ref-Venezuela_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Venezuela-168">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>, North Atlantic Treaty Organization (<a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>) and most UN member countries do not recognise South Ossetia as an independent state. The <i>de facto</i> republic governed by the secessionist government held a second <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_independence_referendum,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="South Ossetian independence referendum, 2006">independence referendum</a><sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated4_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated4-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> on 12 November 2006, after its first referendum in 1992 was not recognised by most governments as valid.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> According to the Tskhinvali election authorities, the referendum turned out a majority for independence from Georgia where 99% of South Ossetian voters supported independence and the turnout for the vote was 95%.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was monitored by a team of 34 international <a href="/wiki/Election_observers" class="mw-redirect" title="Election observers">observers</a> from Germany, Austria, Poland, Sweden and other countries at 78 polling stations.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> However, it was not recognised internationally by the UN, European Union, OSCE, NATO and the Russian Federation, given the lack of ethnic Georgian participation and the illegality of such a referendum without recognition from the Georgian government in <a href="/wiki/Tbilisi" title="Tbilisi">Tbilisi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The European Union, OSCE and NATO condemned the referendum.
</p><p>Parallel to the secessionist held referendum and elections, to <a href="/wiki/Eduard_Kokoity" title="Eduard Kokoity">Eduard Kokoity</a>, the then <a href="/wiki/President_of_South_Ossetia" title="President of South Ossetia">President of South Ossetia</a>, the Ossetian opposition movement (<a href="/wiki/People_of_South_Ossetia_for_Peace" title="People of South Ossetia for Peace">People of South Ossetia for Peace</a>) organised their own elections contemporaneously in Georgian-controlled areas within South Ossetia, in which Georgian and some Ossetian inhabitants of the region voted in favour of <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Sanakoyev" title="Dmitry Sanakoyev">Dmitry Sanakoyev</a> as the alternative President of South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-174">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> The alternative elections of Sanakoyev claimed full support of the ethnic Georgian population.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 2007, Georgia created the <a href="/wiki/Provisional_Administrative_Entity_of_South_Ossetia" class="mw-redirect" title="Provisional Administrative Entity of South Ossetia">Provisional Administrative Entity of South Ossetia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> staffed by ethnic <a href="/wiki/Ossetians" title="Ossetians">Ossetian</a> members of the separatist movement. <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Sanakoyev" title="Dmitry Sanakoyev">Dmitry Sanakoyev</a> was assigned as the leader of the Entity. It was intended that this provisional administration would negotiate with central Georgian authorities regarding its final status and conflict resolution.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> On 10 May 2007, Sanakoyev was appointed by the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Georgia" title="President of Georgia">President of Georgia</a> as the Head of South Ossetian Provisional Administrative Entity.
</p><p>On 13 July 2007, Georgia set up a state commission, chaired by the Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Zurab_Noghaideli" class="mw-redirect" title="Zurab Noghaideli">Zurab Noghaideli</a>, to develop South Ossetia's autonomous status within the Georgian state. According to the Georgian officials, the status was to be elaborated within the framework of "an all-inclusive dialogue" with all the forces and communities within the Ossetian society.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>South Ossetia, <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Artsakh" title="Republic of Artsakh">Artsakh</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a> are sometimes referred to as post-Soviet "<a href="/wiki/Frozen_conflict" title="Frozen conflict">frozen conflict</a>" zones.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Plans_for_integration_with_the_Russian_Federation">Plans for integration with the Russian Federation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Plans for integration with the Russian Federation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Proposed_Russian_annexation_of_South_Ossetia" title="Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia">Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia</a></div>
<p>On 30 August 2008, Tarzan Kokoity, the Deputy Speaker of South Ossetia's parliament, announced that the region would soon be absorbed into Russia, so that South and North Ossetians could live together in one united Russian state.<sup id="cite_ref-times0830_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-times0830-183">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Russian and South Ossetian forces began giving residents in <a href="/wiki/Akhalgori" title="Akhalgori">Akhalgori</a>, the biggest town in the predominantly ethnic Georgian eastern part of South Ossetia, the choice of accepting Russian citizenship or leaving.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> However, <a href="/wiki/Eduard_Kokoity" title="Eduard Kokoity">Eduard Kokoity</a>, the then president of South Ossetia, later stated that South Ossetia would not give up its independence by joining Russia: "We are not going to say no to our independence, which has been achieved at the expense of many lives; South Ossetia has no plans to join Russia." <a href="/wiki/Civil_Georgia" title="Civil Georgia">Civil Georgia</a> has said that this statement contradicts previous ones made by Kokoity earlier that day, when he indicated that South Ossetia would join <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetia%E2%80%93Alania" title="North Ossetia–Alania">North Ossetia</a> in the Russian Federation.<sup id="cite_ref-times0830_183-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-times0830-183">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The South Ossetian and Russian presidents signed an "alliance and integration" treaty on 18 March 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-integration_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-integration-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> The agreement includes provisions to incorporate the South Ossetian military into Russia's armed forces, integrate the customs service of South Ossetia into that of Russia's, and commit Russia to paying state worker salaries in South Ossetia at rates equal to those in the <a href="/wiki/North_Caucasus_Federal_District" class="mw-redirect" title="North Caucasus Federal District">North Caucasus Federal District</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Associated_Press" title="Associated Press">Associated Press</a> described the treaty as calling for "nearly full integration" and compared it to a 2014 agreement between Russia and <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-integration_186-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-integration-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> The Georgian Foreign Ministry described the signing of the treaty as "actual annexation" of the disputed region by Russia, and the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> said they would not recognise it.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In another move towards integration with the Russian Federation, South Ossetian President <a href="/wiki/Leonid_Tibilov" title="Leonid Tibilov">Leonid Tibilov</a> proposed in December 2015 a name change to South Ossetia–Alania – in analogy with <a href="/wiki/North_Ossetia%E2%80%93Alania" title="North Ossetia–Alania">North Ossetia–Alania</a>, a Russian federal subject. Tibilov furthermore suggested holding a referendum on joining the Russian Federation prior to April 2017, which would lead to a united "Ossetia–Alania".<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> In April 2016, Tibilov said he intended to hold the referendum before August of that year.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> However, on 30 May, Tibilov postponed the referendum until after the presidential election due in April 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> At the <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_name_change_referendum,_2017" class="mw-redirect" title="South Ossetian name change referendum, 2017">name-change referendum</a>, nearly 80 per cent of those who voted endorsed the name-change, while the presidential race was won by <a href="/wiki/Anatoliy_Bibilov" class="mw-redirect" title="Anatoliy Bibilov">Anatoliy Bibilov</a> – against the incumbent, Tibilov, who had been supported by Moscow and who, unlike Bibilov, was ready to heed Moscow's wish for the integration referendum not be held any time soon.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 30 March 2022, President <a href="/wiki/Anatoly_Bibilov" title="Anatoly Bibilov">Anatoly Bibilov</a> announced his intention to begin legal proceedings in the near future to integration with the Russian Federation,<sup id="cite_ref-PretensesDropped_139-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PretensesDropped-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> although he subsequently lost the presidency in the <a href="/wiki/2022_South_Ossetian_presidential_election" title="2022 South Ossetian presidential election">2022 South Ossetian presidential election</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_on_occupied_territories_of_Georgia">Law on occupied territories of Georgia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Law on occupied territories of Georgia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Occupied_territories_of_Georgia" class="mw-redirect" title="Occupied territories of Georgia">Occupied territories of Georgia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf/page1-170px-Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf/page1-255px-Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf/page1-340px-Law_of_Georgia_on_Occupied_Territories_2008.pdf.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1275" data-file-height="1650" /></a><figcaption>"The Law of Georgia on Occupied Territories" (in Georgian), 23 October 2008</figcaption></figure>
<p>In late October 2008, President Saakashvili signed into law legislation on the occupied territories passed by the Georgian Parliament. The law covers the breakaway regions of Abkhazia and Tskhinvali (territories of former South Ossetian Autonomous Oblast).<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-lawoccupied_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lawoccupied-196">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> The law spells out restrictions on free movement to, economic activity and concluding real estate transactions in these territories. In particular, according to the law, foreign citizens should enter the two breakaway regions only through Georgia. Entry into Abkhazia should be carried out from <a href="/wiki/Zugdidi_Municipality" title="Zugdidi Municipality">Zugdidi Municipality</a> and into South Ossetia from <a href="/wiki/Gori_Municipality" title="Gori Municipality">Gori Municipality</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/S10_highway_(Georgia)" title="S10 highway (Georgia)">main road</a> leading to South Ossetia from the rest of Georgia passes through the Gori Municipality. However, this road is closed in both directions at <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ergneti&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ergneti (page does not exist)">Ergneti</a> since 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> The main crossing point that remained open for Georgians and South Ossetians, to the <a href="/wiki/Akhalgori_Municipality" title="Akhalgori Municipality">Akhalgori</a> district, has been closed by South Ossetia since 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> Furthermore, the South Ossetian authorities only allow entry of foreigners "through the territory of the Russian Federation".<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Georgian legislation, however, also lists "special" cases in which entry into the breakaway regions will not be regarded as illegal. It stipulates that a special permit on entry into the breakaway regions can be issued if the trip there "serves Georgia's state interests; peaceful resolution of the conflict; de-occupation or humanitarian purposes."<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> The law also bans any type of economic activity – entrepreneurial or non- entrepreneurial, if such activities require permits, licences or registration in accordance with Georgian legislation. It also bans air, sea and railway communications and international transit via the regions, mineral exploration and money transfers.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The provision covering economic activities is retroactive, going back to 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The law says that the Russian Federation – the state which has carried out military occupation – is fully responsible for the violation of human rights in Abkhazia and South Ossetia. The Russian Federation, according to the document, is also responsible for compensation of material and moral damage inflicted on Georgian citizens, stateless persons and foreign citizens, who are in Georgia and enter the occupied territories with appropriate permits.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> The law also says that de facto state agencies and officials operating in the occupied territories are regarded by Georgia as illegal.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> The law will remain in force until "the full restoration of Georgian jurisdiction" over the breakaway regions is realised.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 2009, during the opening ceremony of a new Georgian Embassy building in <a href="/wiki/Kyiv" title="Kyiv">Kyiv</a>, Ukraine, Georgian President <a href="/wiki/Mikheil_Saakashvili" title="Mikheil Saakashvili">Mikheil Saakashvili</a> stated that residents of South Ossetia and <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a> could also use its facilities: "I would like to assure you, my dear friends, that this is your home, as well, and here you will always be able to find support and understanding".<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li>Second level: Towns / cities</li></ul></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title" style="text-align:left; background:lavender;"><a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_South_Ossetia" title="Foreign relations of South Ossetia">Foreign relations</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content" style="padding-top:0;text-align:left;">
<ul><li>Ministry of Foreign Affairs
<dl><dd><a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(South_Ossetia)" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (South Ossetia)">Minister</a>: <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Medoyev" title="Dmitry Medoyev">Dmitry Medoyev</a></dd></dl></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Abkhazia_and_South_Ossetia" title="International recognition of Abkhazia and South Ossetia">International recognition</a></li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">Georgian–Ossetian conflict</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Proposed_Russian_annexation_of_South_Ossetia" title="Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia">Proposed Russian annexation of South Ossetia</a></li></ul>

<ul><li><a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_passport" title="South Ossetian passport">Passport</a></li>

<hr />
<div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;"><a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_missions_of_South_Ossetia" class="mw-redirect" title="Diplomatic missions of South Ossetia">Diplomatic missions of</a> / <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_South_Ossetia" title="List of diplomatic missions in South Ossetia">in South Ossetia</a></div></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below">
<div class="hlist" style="text-align:center;font-weight:normal;">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Outline_of_political_science#Politics_by_region" title="Outline of political science">Other countries</a></li></ul>
</div></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Politics_of_South_Ossetia" title="Template:Politics of South Ossetia"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Politics_of_South_Ossetia" title="Template talk:Politics of South Ossetia"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Politics_of_South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Until the <a href="/wiki/2008_South_Ossetia_war" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 South Ossetia war">armed conflict of August 2008</a>, South Ossetia consisted of a checkerboard of Georgian-inhabited and Ossetian-inhabited towns and villages.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The largely Ossetian capital city of <a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a> and most of the other Ossetian-inhabited communities were governed by the separatist government, while the Georgian-inhabited villages and towns were administered by the Georgian government. This close proximity and the intermixing of the two communities has made the <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">Georgian–Ossetian conflict</a> particularly dangerous, since any attempt to create an ethnically pure territory would involve <a href="/wiki/Population_transfer" title="Population transfer">population transfers</a> on a large scale.
</p><p>The political dispute has yet to be resolved and the South Ossetian separatist authorities govern the region with effective independence from Tbilisi. Although talks have been held periodically between the two sides, little progress was made under the government of <a href="/wiki/Eduard_Shevardnadze" title="Eduard Shevardnadze">Eduard Shevardnadze</a> (1993–2003). His successor <a href="/wiki/Mikheil_Saakashvili" title="Mikheil Saakashvili">Mikheil Saakashvili</a> (elected 2004) made the reassertion of Georgian governmental authority a political priority. Having successfully put an end to the <i>de facto</i> independence of the southwestern province of <a href="/wiki/Ajaria" class="mw-redirect" title="Ajaria">Ajaria</a> in May 2004, he pledged to seek a similar solution in South Ossetia. After the 2004 clashes, the Georgian government has intensified its efforts to bring the problem to international attention. On 25 January 2005, President Saakashvili presented a Georgian vision for resolving the South Ossetian conflict at the <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_Assembly_of_the_Council_of_Europe" title="Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe">Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe</a> session in <a href="/wiki/Strasbourg" title="Strasbourg">Strasbourg</a>. Late in October, Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Zurab_Noghaideli" class="mw-redirect" title="Zurab Noghaideli">Zurab Noghaideli</a> presented the Georgian action plan at the OSCE Permanent Council at <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-osce2015_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-osce2015-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> to which the US government and the OSCE expressed their support.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> The South Ossetian <i>de facto</i> authorities reacted by saying the plan is "not realistic" and "contains nothing new for the South Ossetian side".<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> On 6 December, the OSCE Ministerial Council in <a href="/wiki/Ljubljana" title="Ljubljana">Ljubljana</a> adopted a resolution supporting the Georgian peace plan.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> Prior to the Ministerial Council, the Russian Foreign Ministry rejected the Georgian plan,<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> saying it differed from the plan Saakashvili presented at the UN General Assembly in September 2004,<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> which was favoured by the South Ossetian side. After the OSCE resolution, the South Ossetian side surprised many with their own initiative reminiscent to the Georgian plan,<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> triggering optimism in Tbilisi.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>According to Article 47 of the South Ossetian <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_South_Ossetia" title="Constitution of South Ossetia">Constitution</a>, the <a href="/wiki/President_of_South_Ossetia" title="President of South Ossetia">president</a> of the Republic of South Ossetia is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and head of the executive branch of government. The president of the republic is elected for five years by direct popular vote, with a maximum of two consecutive terms for the same person. South Ossetia's legislative body is the unicameral <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_South_Ossetia" title="Parliament of South Ossetia">parliament</a>, which consists of 34 members who are elected by popular vote for five years in a mixed system of 17 <a href="/wiki/Single-member_district" title="Single-member district">single-member</a> constituencies and 17 delegates elected through <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a> (article 57).<sup id="cite_ref-constitutionrso_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitutionrso-217">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 24 May 2022, <a href="/wiki/Alan_Gagloev" title="Alan Gagloev">Alan Gagloev</a> has been the president of the republic, winning the contested <a href="/wiki/2022_South_Ossetian_presidential_election" title="2022 South Ossetian presidential election">election</a> of the then incumbent <a href="/wiki/Anatoly_Bibilov" title="Anatoly Bibilov">Anatoly Bibilov</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_South_Ossetia" title="Armed Forces of South Ossetia">Armed Forces of South Ossetia</a></div>
<p>South Ossetia's armed forces in 2017 were partially incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Armed_Forces" title="Russian Armed Forces">Russian Armed Forces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> The Russian armed forces have established the <a href="/wiki/4th_Guards_Military_Base" title="4th Guards Military Base">4th Guards Military Base</a> in South Ossetia, which is based in <a href="/wiki/Tskhinvali" title="Tskhinvali">Tskhinvali</a>, with training sites north from the city (Dzartsem) and near <a href="/wiki/Java_(town)" title="Java (town)">Java</a>, where a branch of the base is hosted at the village Ugardanta for the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Airborne_Forces" title="Russian Airborne Forces">Russian Airborne Forces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Furthermore, Russia has established nearly 20 so called "militarized border guard bases"<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> near the boundary line with Tbilisi controlled Georgia, which fall under the command and responsibility of the Russian FSB and are tasked with enforcing a "state border" between South Ossetia and Georgia.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 3,000–3,500 Russian servicemen are deployed in South Ossetia, while an estimated 1,500 FSB personnel are deployed at the border guard bases.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> According to South Ossetian de facto authorities, about 450 South Ossetian citizens are employed at the 4th Russian Military Base.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 March 2022, president Bibilov said that South Ossetia had sent troops to aid Russia during the <a href="/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine">2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine</a>, stating his troops "understand perfectly that they are going to defend Russia, they are going to defend Ossetia too".<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> A large fraction of these troops would desert and hitchhike back to South Ossetia. Bibilov later said that the deserters would face no punishment.<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg/220px-Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="250" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg/330px-Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg/440px-Caucasus-ethnic_en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="977" data-file-height="1111" /></a><figcaption>Ethnic map of the Caucasus from 1995 <a href="/wiki/Ossetians" title="Ossetians">Ossetians</a> live in North and South Ossetia</figcaption></figure>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:0; top:0; border-width:0 200px 200px 0; border-color:gray"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 1987.7609902728px; border-left-color:cyan"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:cyan"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:cyan"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 1050.8418095759px; border-left-color:navy"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:navy"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:navy"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 595.02425868362px; border-left-color:Red"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Red"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Red"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 136.55264628117px; border-left-color:yellow"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:yellow"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:yellow"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>2015 census
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:yellow; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Ossetians (89.94%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Red; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Georgians (7.41%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:navy; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Russians (1.14%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:cyan; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Armenians (0.71%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:gray; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;other (0.81%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>Before the <a href="/wiki/Georgian%E2%80%93Ossetian_conflict" title="Georgian–Ossetian conflict">Georgian–Ossetian conflict</a> roughly two-thirds of the population of South Ossetia was <a href="/wiki/Ossetians" title="Ossetians">Ossetian</a> and 25–30% was <a href="/wiki/Georgian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Georgian people">Georgian</a>. The eastern quarter of South Ossetia, around the town and district of <a href="/wiki/Akhalgori" title="Akhalgori">Akhalgori</a>, was predominantly Georgian, while the center and west were predominantly Ossetian. Much of the mountainous north is sparsely inhabited (see map at <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_the_Caucasus" title="Languages of the Caucasus">Languages of the Caucasus</a>).
</p><p>The Georgian 2002 census was incomplete with regard to South Ossetia, as it only took place in areas under effective Georgian control at the time. This concerned the Georgian populated areas of Akhalgori district and the Georgian communities around Tskhinvali, in the Patara Liachvi and Didi Liakhvi valleys.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> although according to some estimates there were 47,000 ethnic Ossetians and 17,500 ethnic Georgians in South Ossetia in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg/220px-Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg/330px-Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg/440px-Palm_Sunday_procession_in_Tskhinvali.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="514" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Palm_Sunday" title="Palm Sunday">Palm Sunday</a> procession in Tskhinvali</figcaption></figure>
<p>2009 population estimate: During the war, according to Georgian officials, 15,000 Georgians moved to Georgia proper; South Ossetian officials indicate that 30,000 Ossetians fled to North Ossetia, and a total of 500 citizens of South Ossetia were killed.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-233">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the 2015 census conducted by the South Ossetian authorities, the region's total population was 53,532, including 48,146 Ossetians (89.9%), 3,966 Georgians (7.4%), and 610 Russians. The Georgian authorities have questioned the accuracy of these data.<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Estimates based on official birth rates and school attendance suggest it may have been around 39,000 inhabitants, and an independent estimate from 2009 put the population at 26,000.<sup id="cite_ref-3DCFTAs_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3DCFTAs-236">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the South Ossetian Statistical agency the population estimate was 56,520 per 1 January 2022. Of these, 33,054 lived in Tskhinvali.<sup id="cite_ref-2021pop_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2021pop-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy" title="Eastern Orthodoxy">Eastern Orthodoxy</a> is the major religion practised in South Ossetia, by Ossetians, Georgians and Russians.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg/220px-Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg/330px-Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg/440px-Dzuarikau-Tskhinval.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Dzuarikau%E2%80%93Tskhinvali_pipeline" title="Dzuarikau–Tskhinvali pipeline">Dzuarikau–Tskhinvali pipeline</a>, delivering <a href="/wiki/Natural_gas" title="Natural gas">natural gas</a> from Russia to South Ossetia, went online in 2009.</figcaption></figure>
<p>South Ossetia's economy is primarily agricultural, although less than 10% of South Ossetia's land area is cultivated. Cereals, fruit and vines are the major produce. Forestry and cattle industries are also maintained. A number of industrial facilities also exist, particularly around the capital, Tskhinvali. Following the war in the 1990s, South Ossetia struggled economically. South Ossetian GDP was estimated at US$15&#160;million (US$250 per capita) in a work published in 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, the Administration of South Ossetia estimated its GDP to be nearly 100 million US dollars.<sup id="cite_ref-investrso.su_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-investrso.su-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> South Ossetia's <a href="/wiki/Poverty_threshold" title="Poverty threshold">poverty threshold</a> stood at 3,062 rubles a month in the fourth quarter of 2007, or 23.5 per cent below Russia's average, while South Ossetians have incomparably smaller incomes.<sup id="cite_ref-delyagin_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delyagin-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The majority of the population survives on subsistence farming. The South Ossetian authorities planned to improve finances by boosting the local production of flour and thus reducing the need for flour imports. For this purpose, the area planted with wheat was increased tenfold in 2008 from 130 hectares to 1,500 hectares. The wheat harvest in 2008 was expected to be 2,500 tons of grain. The South Ossetian Agriculture ministry also imported some tractors in 2008, and was expecting delivery of more farm machinery in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-delyagin_242-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delyagin-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Before the <a href="/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War" title="Russo-Georgian War">2008 Russo-Georgian War</a>, South Ossetia's industry consisted of 22 small factories, with a total production of 61.6&#160;million rubles in 2006. In 2007, only 7 factories were functioning. In March 2009, it was reported that most of the production facilities were standing idle and were in need of repairs. Even successful factories have a shortage of workers, are in debt and have a shortage of working capital.<sup id="cite_ref-delyagin_242-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delyagin-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> One of the largest local enterprises is the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Emalprovod&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Emalprovod (page does not exist)">Emalprovod</a> factory, which has 130 employees.<sup id="cite_ref-delyagin_242-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delyagin-242">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, after the 2008 war, Georgia cut off supplies of electricity to the Akhalgori region, which aggravated the socio-economic situation in that area.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By the end of 2021 the number of employed people was determined at 20,734 while 2,449 persons were registered as unemployed, on a total working age population of 34,308 (men 18–65, women 18–60).<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Virtually the only significant economic asset that South Ossetia possesses is control of the <a href="/wiki/Roki_Tunnel" title="Roki Tunnel">Roki Tunnel</a> that is used to link Russia and Georgia, from which the South Ossetian government reportedly obtained a large part of its budget by levying customs duties on freight traffic before the <a href="/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War" title="Russo-Georgian War">war</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the 2008 war South Ossetia and its economy is critically dependent on Russian economic assistance,<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> and a year after the war former President Edurard Kokoity was grateful for the Russian aid for reconstruction work.<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> According to reports Russian donations made up nearly 99% of the budget of South Ossetia by 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> By 2021 this has been reduced to 83%.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup>
A new backup power transmission line running from Russia to South Ossetia was put into operation in November 2021 helping to ensure uninterrupted power supply to the region. It cost more than 1.3 billion rubles ($17m) to construct and was built within the framework of the Russian Investment program in South Ossetia.<sup id="cite_ref-253" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-253">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2016, Armenia tried to persuade Georgia to open a transit route between Georgia and South Ossetia, but Georgia refused.<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The socio-economic development program of South Ossetia for the 2022–2025 period is financed by Russia.The Aim of the program is for South Ossetia to reach the socio-economic indicators of the North Caucasus Federal District in 2025.<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ossetian_music" title="Ossetian music">Ossetian music</a> and <a href="/wiki/Women_in_South_Ossetia" title="Women in South Ossetia">Women in South Ossetia</a></div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The country's principal university is <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetian_State_University" title="South Ossetian State University">South Ossetian State University</a> in Tskhinvali.<sup id="cite_ref-uwn_257-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-uwn-257">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> After the Russo-Georgian War in 2008, education officials attempted to place most university-bound students from South Ossetia in Russian <a href="/wiki/Post-secondary_education" class="mw-redirect" title="Post-secondary education">post-secondary education</a> institutions.<sup id="cite_ref-uwn_257-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-uwn-257">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_Holidays">Public Holidays</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Public Holidays">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Ossetia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-5">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><div><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Ossetian_language" title="Ossetian language">Ossetian</a>: <span lang="os">Хуссар Ирыстон</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Ossetian-language romanization"><i lang="os-Latn">Khussar Iryston</i></span></li><li><a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a>: <span lang="ru">Южная Осетия</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Russian" title="Romanization of Russian">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Russian-language romanization"><i lang="ru-Latn">Yuzhnaya Osetiya</i></span></li><li><a href="/wiki/Georgian_language" title="Georgian language">Georgian</a>&#58; <span title="Georgian-language text"><span lang="ka" style="font-style: normal;">სამხრეთი ოსეთი</span></span>, <span style="font-size:85%;">romanised:</span> <span title="Georgian-language romanization"><i lang="ka-Latn">Samxreti Oseti</i></span></li></ul></div></span>
</li>

<li id="cite_note-89"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-89">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>"The local ‘security’ institutions in Abkhazia, South Ossetia and Transnistria are often headed by Russians or officials who are de facto delegated by state institutions of the Russian Federation. This most often includes staff in the local intelligence services and the defence ministries. Examples of Russians de facto delegated to the secessionist entities include defence ministers Anatoli Barankevich (South Ossetia) and Sultan Sosnaliev (Abkhazia), local intelligence chief Iarovoi (South Ossetia) and Interior Minister <a href="/wiki/Mikhail_Mindzaev" title="Mikhail Mindzaev">Mikhail Mindzaev</a> (South Ossetia)."</i><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup></span>
